## Document 031: 2026-11-05_［みずほ経済インサイト］欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道_c421a30a

欧州大規模デモが問う財政再建の道 
金融市場が動揺するリスクは当面残存する見通し

○ このところ欧州ではインフレに伴う生活苦や公共サービスの劣化等を背景に各地で大規模抗議活
動が発生している 
○ 大規模デモは緊縮を伴う財政健全化を難しくする要因となる。財政懸念や政情不安は国債利回り
の上昇やユーロ安を招いており、経済への悪影響も懸念される 
○ 来年にかけて、欧州では複数の重要な国政選挙が予定される中、政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金
融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存するだろう

１．欧州で広がる大規模抗議活動 
このところ、欧州では大規模デモが多発している（図表1）。フランスでは、9月以降、教員不足や
学校施設の不備など、教育環境の改善を求める高校生の抗議活動が続いていた。こうした中で、10月
半ばには、物価高への対策や公共サービスの改善を求める市民も加わり、抗議活動は全国規模に拡大
した。 
大規模デモが発生しているのはフランスだけではない。フランスのデモに一部触発される形でベル
図表 1 9 月以降に欧州で発生した主な抗議活動 図表2 EU：生活苦の割合（左）と 
自国が抱える課題（右）

### Page 2

2

ギーやイタリア等でも抗議活動が発生している。また、スペインでは、高齢女性が家賃を支払えずに
強制退去させられたことをきっかけに、住宅不足や住宅価格・家賃高騰等に対する不満が爆発する形
で大規模な抗議活動が発生し、これが遠因となりサンチェス首相は議会の解散に追い込まれた。 
各地で発生する大規模抗議活動の背景には、近年のインフレによる生活苦やこれを受けた政府への
不満がある1。EUにおいて、生活苦を訴える人の割合は低中所得層を中心に2022年以降高まっている
ほか、自国が直面する課題としてインフレや経済状況を挙げる人の割合も高い（図表2）。これらの要
因が、自国政府に対する信頼感の低迷につながっている（図表3）。 
本稿では、生活改善を求めて各国で広がる抗議活動が経済や財政、金融市場に与える影響について
考察する。 
２．大規模抗議活動は財政健全化を阻む要因に 
一連の抗議活動は、2018年にフランスで発生した黄色いベスト運動を想起させる2。当時の参加者は、
自動車に常備が義務付けられている黄色い安全ベストを着用し、燃料税の増税や生活費高騰に抗議し
た。購買力の低下や政府への不満が背景にある点は当時と共通している。異なるのは、政府の追加的
な財政支出の余地が、当時と比べてさらに狭まっていることである。 
欧州債務危機が起きた2010年代前半以降、順調に進捗してきた政府債務の削減は、2020年代に入る
と足踏みしはじめた。パンデミック時の大規模給付やウクライナ戦争後のエネルギー高対策など、危
機対応の財政出動が頻発したためだ。黄色いベスト運動が発生した2018年と比べて政府債務が増加し
たEU加盟国は、27カ国中18カ国にのぼる（図表4）。 
加えて、足元では防衛費増額や社会保障費・利払い負担の拡大など、構造的な財政支出圧力も高ま
っている。各国の次年度予算案を踏まえると、財政赤字は当面、高止まりする公算が大きい（図表5）。
安全保障環境の変化やエネルギー価格の高騰を踏まえ、EUは一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連の追加支
出をEU財政ルールの対象外とする柔軟化措置を設けている。しかし、当該措置を適用しているドイツ
図表3 EU市民の自国政府への信頼度 図表4 EU加盟国の一般政府債務残高の変化 
（2018年～2025年）

### Page 3

3

やベルギーでは、特例対象の追加支出を除いてもなお、財政赤字をGDP比3％に抑制するEUの基準を
超過する見込みである。それでもベルギーでは、連立与党間の予算協議が決裂し、首相が辞意を表明
する事態に陥った。 
フランスを起点に広がった抗議活動は、緊縮を伴う財政健全化の難しさを浮き彫りにしている。2018
年の黄色いベスト運動では、マクロン政権が燃料税増税の撤回や所得支援策の拡充に踏み切ったこと
で、抗議活動が漸く沈静化に向かった。今回は、11月に新たなデモやストライキが計画されるなど、
いまだ抗議活動が収束する見通しは立っていない。各国政府は事態の長期化を回避するため、家計支
援や公共サービス拡充などの対応を迫られる可能性があり、財政拡張圧力が一段と強まることが懸念
される。 
３．政治・財政を巡る不安が欧州金融市場を動揺させるリスクは当面残存 
金融市場は、欧州主要国の財政状況に対する警戒を強めている。 
フランスをはじめとする高債務国では、政治や財政を巡る混乱を嫌気して、10年債利回りが上昇し
ている（図表6）。2010年代の欧州債務危機を受けてECB（欧州中央銀行）は、条件付きで対象国の国
債を無制限に購入可能なOMT（Outright Monetary Transactions）や経済の基礎的条件では正当化されな
い国債市場の混乱が金融政策の波及を阻害する場合に発動可能なTPI （ Transmission Protection 
Instrument）などの市場安定化措置3を整備してきた。当時よりセーフティーネットが整備されている
こともあり、足元の国債利回り上昇が欧州債務危機時のような金融危機につながる可能性は現時点で
は低い。 
とはいえ、金利の上昇は景気下押しや、さらなる財政悪化の要因となる。金利上昇幅が大きいフラ
ンスを例に足元の利回り上昇幅を踏まえて試算すると、設備投資・住宅投資が1年後に3％程度減少す
るとの結果が得られる（図表7）。金利上昇やそれに伴う景気減速は、利払い費の増加や税収の減少を
図表5 主要国の財政収支実績と見通し 図表6 ユーロ圏の高債務国と 
ドイツの10年債利回り

きなかった国。数値は成立前の政府予算案ベース。

### Page 4

4

通じて、財政をさらに悪化させる要因にもなる。 
各国の政情不安や財政への懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営をも左右する可能性がある。足元では、ユ
ーロが主要通貨に対して下落している（図表8）。ユーロ安は、輸入物価の上昇を通じてインフレ率を
押し上げる要因となる。足元のユーロ下落幅を前提に、時系列モデルを用いてユーロ圏のインフレ率
への影響を試算すると、総合インフレ率は0.3％Pt程度、コアインフレ率は0.1％Pt程度押し上げられる
との結果が得られた（図表9）。ユーロ圏では、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴うエネルギー価格の高騰を背
景に、直近でもインフレ率が加速している。先行きユーロ安圧力が強い状況が続けば、インフレ率に
一段の上昇圧力がかかる可能性がある。しかし、ECBはインフレ圧力が一段と強まっても、状況によ
っては追加利上げに踏み切りにくくなることが想定される。利上げがさらなる長期金利の上昇、国債
市場の不安定化を招くおそれがあるためだ。このように政情不安や財政懸念は、ECBの金融政策運営
を困難にする要因にもなりかねない。 
各国の財政や政治情勢に対する金融市場の懸念は当面残存する可能性が高い。欧州では、今年11月
にスペイン議会選挙、来年にはフランス大統領選挙やイタリア議会選挙など、重要な国政選挙が相次
いで予定されている。大規模デモの発生に加えて選挙も控える中、各国政府は国民に負担を求める緊
縮的な財政政策を例年以上に打ち出しにくくなっている。また、各国の選挙では極右政党やポピュリ
スト政党の勢力拡大が予想され、選挙後の政治はより一層不安定化するリスクがある。このほか、コ
ロナ禍からの経済復興を目的に設立されたEU復興基金の受け取りが終了することも、来年以降の財政
懸念を強める要因になるだろう。同基金の配分が手厚かった南欧や中東欧諸国は、自国の財政負担を
抑えながら公共投資などを通じて経済成長を支えてきたが、来年以降はこうした効果が徐々に剥落す
ることになる。

### Page 5

5

以上のように、2020年代に入ってから、コロナ禍におけるサプライチェーン混乱、ロシアのウクラ
イナ侵攻やイラン情勢の緊迫によるエネルギー高が立て続けに起きてインフレが継続する中、欧州各
国で有権者の生活コスト上昇に対する不満が強まっている。一方、これらの緊急事態に対応するため
財政出動を繰り返してきたことに加え、高齢化に伴う社会保障費の増加、地政学リスクの高まりを背
景とした防衛費増強の必要性、利払い費の増大に直面しており、各国の財政余力は著しく低下してい
る。来年にかけて予定されている主要国の選挙では、極右・ポピュリスト政党の勢力拡大が予想され
ており、政治の混乱に拍車がかかる懸念もある。欧州の政治情勢や財政状況に対する不安が来年にか
けて沈静化するとは考えにくく、国債市場や為替市場が動揺するリスクに引き続き留意する必要があ
ろう。

## Document 089: 2026年7-9月期GDP予測（1次速報） ～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期GDP予測（1次速報） ～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/3 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅ながら４四半期連続のプラス成長を予想、景気回復基調は維持 
11 月16 日に公表される2026 年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋
0.1％）と予測する。４四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
一方、設備投資が高い伸びになることが下支えになる見込みである。設備投資の伸びには特殊要
因も一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。個人消費が弱含むなかでも
プラス成長を維持するとみられることは、企業部門の底堅さが景気を下支えしていることを示し
ている。過去３四半期に比較的高い成長が続いた後であることも踏まえれば、今回の減速をもっ
て景気の基調が弱まったと判断する必要はないだろう。家計部門の弱さは懸念材料だが、企業部
門の底堅さは維持されており、景気は引き続き緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
７-９月期のＧＤＰを評価する際には、原油輸入と公的在庫の動きをセットで捉える必要があ
る。４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を
通じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油
が放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt
（前期比年率寄与度▲2.1％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げ
は、公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じるとみられる。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって
原油輸入が回復するため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因になる。一方、輸入の回復に
伴って国家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小す
るとみられる。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.4％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋1.5％
Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し上げることが予想される。

1 10 月 30 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果
を踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げと公的在庫変動による押し上げは、ともに原油の供給環境が改
善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一
体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクが低下していることである。
ＧＤＰ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用
可能な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むし
ろ前向きに捉えるべきだろう。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減少を予想する。８～９月に各地で相次いだ大雨や台風などの
天候不順が外出機会やサービス消費を抑制した可能性がある。また、４-６月期には自動車やエア
コンなどの耐久財販売が増加したが、７-９月期にはこうした一時的な押し上げ効果が剥落した。
加えて、物価高による節約志向が根強いなか、食料品や日用品などの非耐久財消費にも弱さがみ
られる。高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境は下支え要因となっているものの、天候不順という一時的
な要因もあって個人消費は総じて力強さを欠く結果になったとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と、高い伸びを予想する。４-６月期には特許権の海外譲渡に伴う研
究開発投資の減少という特殊要因があったが、７-９月期はその影響が剥落することが押し上げ要
因になった面はある。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を除いても設備投資は明確に増加したと考え
られ、底堅い動きとなっている。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応した省力化
投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資への需要は根強く、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強い。設備投
資計画も高い伸びとなっており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断してよ
いだろう。 
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れとなる見
込みである。両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。 
輸出は前期比＋1.2％と４四半期連続の増加を予想する。４-６月期に大口要因によって研究開
発サービスの受取が急増した分が剥落することで、サービス輸出が減少する一方、財輸出が高い
伸びとなり、財貨・サービス輸出全体では増加を維持したとみられる。半導体製造装置などの資
本財輸出が好調だった。地域別では、ＥＵ向けが低調だった一方、ＡＳＥＡＮ、ＮＩＥｓ向けの
資本財輸出が伸びている。また、４-６月期に急減した中東向け輸出が持ち直したことも押し上げ
につながっている。 
輸入は前期比＋3.3％と大幅な増加が予想される。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が
大幅に減少していたが、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄
与度は前期比▲0.3％Pt（前期比年率▲1.3％Pt）になったと予想される。 
ただし、この外需寄与度のマイナスは、資源輸入の回復に伴う輸入の増加によって生じるもの

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
である。その裏側では公的在庫変動が前期比＋0.4％ポイント、前期比年率では＋1.5％ポイント
の大幅プラス寄与になると見込まれる。両者は併せて評価する必要がある。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.1％Ptと予想する。製品在庫はプラスが予想される一方、流通
在庫の下押しが大きくなるとみられる。 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.9％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因
となっており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。また、公共投資も同▲1.5％と減少を
予想するほか、政府消費についても、４-６月期の高い伸びの反動から前期比▲0.3％と減少を見
込む。

## Document 126: 261110_daiwa_m31

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_daiwa_m31.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following on our results Memo, we lower our forecasts and 
TP for M31, as we factor in its still suboptimal scale and delayed 
employment bonus in 3Q26 that weigh on its trajectory of a bottom-line 
turnaround. That said, we remain comfortable with our Buy (1) call on the 
stock as we expect M31’s turnaround, kicking off from 3Q26 despite at a 
small scale, to sustain into 2027 with meaningful incremental builds, 
supported by its conversion from license to royalty revenue paying off, and 
broadened footprint into advanced technologies for price accretion and into 
diversified foundry, IDM, and fabless customers for volume accretion.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes. We lower our 2026-27E EPS for 
M31 by 14-43% to embed its still suboptimal scale and opex overhang in 
our model. Despite the cuts, its bottom-line turnaround met our expectation, 
and we expect the turnaround to sustain with meaningful incremental builds 
sequentially through 2027 to improve market confidence. We forecast its 
4Q26 top line to rebound by 23% QoQ (+12% YoY) for EPS to reach 
TWD3.31 (TWD0.35 in 3Q26), setting a good start for M31’s 2027 outlook 
and warranting a strong 213% EPS CAGR over 2026-28E.

Turnaround confirmed. While prolonged license project recognitions 
delayed M31’s turnaround, depicted in our previous Flash, we believe its 
bumpy operations over the past 2 years is finally coming to an end, 
evidenced by management’s comments of QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26 
with EPS expanding to cover accumulated losses YTD. M31 has actively 
invested over the past years enriching its IP portfolio, accumulating more 
than 2,000 license projects since 2012 (chart on p.2), and setting a good 
foundation for converting them to royalty revenue. It aims for the royalty 
revenue to outgrow license revenue in the next few quarters, after years of 
“cooking”. Meanwhile, two structural growth drivers remain intact for ASP 
and volume accretion: 1) for ASP, a growing number of high-value projects 
from advanced process nodes to enhance M31’s IP portfolio for ASP 
accretion, and 2) for volume, an increased diversification of customer base 
globally to broaden its addressable markets for volume accretion.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we lower our 12M TP 
for M31 to TWD550 (previous: TWD645), still based on our 4-quarter 
forward PER of 45x. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with key downside risks 
of silicon inflation and geopolitical overhang leading to global inflation and 
AI capex cut to delay M31’s royalty ramps.

How we differ: Our 2027E EPS is 52% above the consensus, likely due to 
our more positive view on royalty revenue ramp.

10 November 2026

Structural turnaround round the corner (III)

➢ Lowering our forecasts to reflect still suboptimal scale… 
➢ … but expecting enriched license projects to bear fruit soon 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating with a lower 12M TP of TWD550

Company profile 
Founded in 2011, M31 is an independent semiconductor IP provider in Taiwan, offering both foundation 
IP for specialty technology nodes and functional IP such as high-speed interface and analogue IP.

Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
M31's board of directors (BoD) has 7 members, including 3 independent directors, who
represent 43% of total directors, higher than the regulatory requirement of at least 20%. Its
Chairman and President are not the same person, but its President is one of the directors. M31
has the Audit Committee, Compensation Committee and Risk Control Committee under the
BoD.
Capital management 1
Although M31 is a young company (founded in 2011 and listed in 2019), its dividend payout ratio
has been over the 75% level for the past 5 years. M31 is a semiconductor IP provider without
onsite production activities, which would require high capex to do so. As such, we view its high
dividend payout ratio to be appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 We do not see any meaningful impact from related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
M31's suppliers are required to comply with the Supplier Management Standards, which was set
by M31 in 2013. In addition, M31 has periodical evaluations for its suppliers and onsite audits to
ensure the quality and implementation of Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR), as well as
Environmental Protection and Safety and Integrity Policies.
E Water & wastewater management 1 M31 is a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, and thus no production activity to affect the environment.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
The company is a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, and thus no production
activity to affect the environment. It still adopts some measures to reduce waste from its
operations, such as recycling and using electronic files to replace paper documents as much as
possible to reduce the use of paper.
E GHG emissions 1 M31 is a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, and thus no production activity to affect the environment.

## Document 131: 261110_gs_ZDT

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

ZDT 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 10%/11% higher than GSe and 
BBG consensus, mainly thanks to the better than expected product 
mix (high margin & high growth sectors accounted for 27% of total 
revenue in 3Q26 vs. our estimate only at 20-25%). In 3Q26, ZDT GM 
was 1.8/2.6ppt higher than GSe/BBG consensus, and was 27.7% up 
4.6ppt QoQ and 5.7ppt YoY, while the annual growth was only 
driven by the better product mix/ new business contribution 
(ABF/optical module/AI PCB), even considering these new 
businesses only accounted for 27% of total revenue.  
OPEX in 3Q26 increased 24% QoQ and 56% YoY due mainly to the 
higher investment in the R&D projects for future new high-end AI 
PCB/ optical transceiver / substrates business, which was the main 
reason for the company to see a 0.7/1.4ppt higher than GSe/BBG 
consensus OPEX to sales ratio in 3Q26.  
In 4Q26, we expect ZDT revenue to go up by ~29% QoQ, mainly due 
to the solid growth from the optical transceiver, substrates, and AI 
PCB businesses, with more new AI projects to ramp in 4Q26. 
Moreover, we expect the company’s GM to further expand to ~30% 
(2.2ppt QoQ or 7.3ppt YoY) thanks to a better product mix (we 
expect high growth/high margin new business will account for 
30-35% of the company’s total revenue in 4Q26, whose average GM 
should be at least 40-45% in 4Q26).  
Investment view 
We remain positive on ZDT’s AI business outlook, and expect the 
acceleration on ZDT’s AI PCB/Substrates business revenue to further 
drive the company’s overall GM level in coming years (we expect the 
two businesses’ average GM could surpass 50% in 2027, thanks to 
the favorable supply demand condition) while we also expect the 
high GM optical transceiver (50%+ GM now) will also see at least 
120% YoY growth in 2027, suggesting a significant GM expansion 
trend for the overall company in coming quarters/years.

Zhen Ding Technology Holding (4958.TW)For the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 3

We believe ZDT will continue to be the key share gainer in the AI PCB market. For AI GPU 
projects, we are seeing ZDT share increase from <5% in 2025 to ~10-15% in 2026, 
which, we believe, will further go up to 30%+ in long term (2028 and beyond), thanks to 
the company’s proactive investment in the R&D on the latest PCB technology, as well as 
the company’s solid investment in the AI grade PCB capacity. For AI ASIC projects, the 
company’s market share is well below 10% for now, and only focus on some minor 
projects, but we do expect the company to become one of the major suppliers for AI 
ASIC projects in coming years, considering the company’s more diversified production 
sites (mainland China + Taiwan + Thailand), while most of the AI ASIC customers are 
asking for non-Greater China capacity now.  
For the optical transceiver business, we expect ZDT to see strong growth (50%+ 
2025-29E CAGR), driven mainly by the ~50% 2026-28E volume demand CAGR and the 
company’s proactive expansion in capacity in coming years (5x more capacity over the 
next two years). Moreover, ZDT currently holds an industry-leading yield of 90%+ for its 
optical module mSAP products (vs. peers only at 60-80% per our industry checks).  
For its ABF substrate business, we believe the company will continue to have the ability 
to increase ABF price, supported by full UTR and strong demand, while the industry in 
our view remains conservative on its capacity expansion plans over the next 1-2 years 
relative to our forecasts. We expect ABF substrate LTA/Spot prices to increase by 
10-15%/20%+ QoQ per quarter from 3Q26 through at least the end of 2027, with 
shortages likely extending into 2028 (see here). Overall, the business condition for ABF 
substrate is improving with price increase and we expect the business, which was loss 
making in 2025, to expand its GM to 40%+/50%+ by end of 2026/27. 
Overall, we reiterate our Buy rating on ZDT, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,070 (from 
NT$1,025).

Earnings revisions 
We revise up our 2026/27/28/29E net income estimate by 6/21/42/49% after seeing 
the solid GM expansion from these high-growth sectors, which we believe will continue 
to be the net income driver in the coming years. We believe the high growth sectors (AI 
PCB, substrates, optical module mSAP) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2028 
and further expand to 60%+ in 2029, with a much higher than company average GM 
(50%+ in average in 2027-28), thus we revise up our 2027/28/29E GM by 
5.4/10.7/12.4ppt vs. previous estimate.

### Page 4

Valuation 
We revise up ZDT 12m TP from NT$1,025 to NT$1,070, still based on our SOTP based 
valuation methodology of (1) 3.5x blended P/B for ABF & BT substrate (in-line with 
upcycle industry average), (2) 15x P/E for high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in 
line with industry average), and (3) 8x P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB 
(in-line with industry downcycle average valuation), and maintain our valuation period at 
4Q26-3Q27, factoring in our earnings revision.

Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China, and has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market 
compared to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% 
ABF substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the 
most from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025E-27E CAGR) 
due to the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. 
We also expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to 
drive ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 50% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We 
see further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due 
to our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

More details on SBC — SBC's 9M26 sales declined by a single digit y/y with stable 
GPM and improving opex. Pre-tax profits rose 40% y/y in 9M26. Yet the higher 
effective tax rate dragged profitability, leading to a  net loss of US$25mn in 9M26. 
Mgmt expects SBC to launch more new products in 2027E which should further 
drive the top-line and earnings recovery.  
Implications — We expect a negative share price reaction to the results print given 
the weaker-than-expected core biz and the SBC loss. Mgmt expects FY26 sales to 
fall 14-15% y/y with 4Q OEM sales seasonally softer than 3Q. New models are due 
from Jan-27 and mgmt expects low-teens growth y/y for FY27E. We will look for 
more detail on the drivers of the expected sales recovery at its 3Q26 results briefing 
next Tuesday, Nov 17.

Merida
Valuation
Our target price for Merida shares of NT$93 is based on a 2H26-1H27E P/E of 15.5x, a 30% discount to its historical average of
22x during pre-Covid years (2014-2019), in view of soft order momentum as a result of slow recovery in bike demand, a volatile
margin trend, and low visibility of SBC.
Risks
Downside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade below our target price include: 1) e-bike adoption slower than

expected; 2) weaker restocking demand; 3) lukewarm sell-through at SBC; and 4) a weaker macro.
Upside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade above our target price include: 1) e-bike adoption faster than expected;
2) stronger restocking demand; 3) stronger sell-through at SBC; and 4) a strong macro.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned

### Page 6

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 177: 261113_daiwa_ Lotes

Source PDF: all report pdf/261113_daiwa_ Lotes.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Lotes’s 3Q26 results, released on 13 November.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. Ongoing gross margin dilution due 
to rising raw material costs and less benefits from the AI server trend has 
been, in our view, the primary drag on Lotes’s share price over this year. 
We believe this trend may reverse now as investors are worried about the 
potential AI capex cuts impacting the overall Taiwan supply chain’s revenue 
outlook in 2028, whereas Lotes only has 3-5% of exposure currently. Also, 
management expects gross margin pressure to start easing with the new 
product mass production (eg, SOCAMM) starting from 4Q26. Second, 
Lotes’s regular server-related products may see less chance for inventory 
correction as its products are not in shortage now. Lastly, its desktop 
business has suffered more than the overall industry’s headwinds (ie, unit 
decline 10-15% YoY) as its desktop products had to downgrade from DDR5 
to DDR4/3 due to component shortages, which further negatively impacted 
profitability. We believe these three headwinds will gradually transform into 
tailwinds once the AI capex starts to slow down. However, if the AI server 
capex trend remains as is, we could also see some upside for the 
company. Lotes is still eager to develop a GPU socket for the Rubin Ultra 
generation, which we believe could become a new growth driver. Besides, 
quick disconnect (QD) and NPO sockets might also become growth drivers. 
While QD only accounts for low single digit of total revenue from 3Q26, we 
expect it to have a chance to increase to c.10% of total revenue in 2027E.

3Q26 profitability missed expectations. Lotes’s 3Q26 earnings came in 
at TWD1.92bn (EPS: TWD17.1), which was 11%/14% lower than our/street 
estimates. Despite revenue largely in-line with expectations, gross margin 
decreased by 0.6pp QoQ to 44.8%, mainly from rising copper and gold 
prices; unfavourable product mix (rising high-speed cable and connect and 
QD) may be another reason. However, operating margin rose 0.9pp QoQ to 
22.9% mainly on the lack of one-off employee stock bonus expense (as 
done in 2Q26) but opex was still higher than the company’s guidance 
(<TWD2.0bn) due to salary increase for its Chinese branches.

What we recommend: We lower 2026-27E EPS by 2-7% to factor in 3Q26 
results and lower profitability recovery assumption but maintain 2028E 
EPS. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call but lower our 12M TP to TWD2,480 (from 
TWD2,850), based on a lower PER of 22x (from 28x; high end of its past-5-
year range of 10-28x), on our 1-year forward EPS, given the weaker market 
sentiment. Key risk: lower-than-expected adoption of server CPU platforms.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E revenues are 5-10% higher than the 
Bloomberg consensus, likely due to our more bullish view on QDs.

13 November 2026

3Q26 review: bottomed-out gross margin

➢ 3Q GM slightly declined on rising material cost but likely bottomed out 
➢ Rising QD, SOCAMM, NPO sockets to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD2,480

Company profile 
Founded in 1986 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2007, Lotes is one of the world’s leading 
connector vendors specialising in CPU socket/ILM and connectors, such as DDR, PCIe, SATA, and 
Type-C, with most of its production capacities at its Guangzhou, Zhongshan and Suzhou plants. Its 
superior manufacturing capability and strong client relationships are the key differentiators that make 
Lotes stand out from its competitors. Since the PC market became saturated from 2015, Lotes has 
focused on penetrating non-PC connector businesses, such as connectors for servers, automotive and 
industrial, and new types of cables (eg, Thunderbolt cable).

### Page 5

5

Lotes (3533 TT): 13 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 13 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Lotes’ board consists of 7 directors, including 3 independent directors. The total number of
directors is slightly lower than other listed companies in our coverage. Lotes held 8 board
meetings in 2025 and the actual attendance rate was 95%.
Although the Chairman and CEO are not the same person, they are, in fact, brothers and the co-
founders of Lotes. It gives us a sense that Lotes is still a family enterprise, which is likely a
negative for the board and executive management. That said, the background of independent
directors is diversified with expertise in technology and management. We see it as a positive for
the board's quality.
Capital management 2
Lotes declared a DPS of TWD41.5 in 2025, representing a payout ratio of 50%. Being a leading
connector vendor for applications such as desktops, notebooks and servers, Lotes grew rapidly
along with its key clients (Intel and AMD) on strong technical capabilities and higher growth rate
from the server market. The solid revenue CAGR in the past decade required higher working
capital and continuous capex to expand its capacity, which could also be the culprit for its slightly
lower payout ratio compared with other electronic component makers. Having said that, we think
Lotes’ higher revenue growth should partially offset the drawback of a lower payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, there were no disclosed related-party sales, while purchases from related parties
amounted to TWD4.4m, representing 0.03% of total cost of sales. We see limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Lotes established its RBA management system in 2010 and requires its suppliers to meet the
standards of no child labour, no forced labour, treating employees with respect and dignity, and
caring for labourers' safety and health. Lotes hosts a yearly supplier conference to promote the
RBA guidelines to its suppliers. In 2025, 187 suppliers participated in the annual supplier
conference. Lotes also requires its suppliers to sign the RBA-related commitment letters. For
better supply chain management, Lotes conducts yearly audits and regular business reviews
and holds the yearly supplier conference as measures. In 2025, Lotes conducted written
evaluations of 110 suppliers and on-site audits of 61 suppliers. In addition, all 76 newly added
suppliers passed its environmental and social responsibility evaluations. Lotes has required new
suppliers to sign the "Supplier Social and Environmental Responsibility Commitment Letter"
since 2010 to extend social responsibility concepts and requirements to the supply chain.
For "green" purchase management, Lotes has adopted the IECQ QC 080000 hazardous
substance process management system. Lotes asks its suppliers to follow the RoHS, HSF, and
REACH guidelines to avoid using hazardous materials. Lotes also conducts conflict minerals due
diligence and requires suppliers to sign non-conflict mineral declarations and complete surveys
tracing mineral sources to smelters. Overall, Lotes believes it has constructed a comprehensive
system covering system certification (e.g., ISO 9001), product design, supply chain
management, and production techniques to ensure that the entire manufacturing process, from
raw materials to end products, follows green principles. All products from Lotes are now 100%
compliant with RoHS standards.
S Data security 2
Lotes has established a team to implement data security regulations, planning and maintenance.
Moreover, it monitors vulnerabilities in its information systems each year through regular
vulnerability scanning, access control reviews and information security audits. The company
reported no losses resulting from information security incidents in 2025.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

力旺(3529)：2H26 權利金大幅成長、Security 長期機會大 
建議：維持買進

一、2026 年前三季權利金營收
+13.4%YOY，PUF-based 權利
金+927%YOY 
 2026 前三季權利金營收 21.49 億元，+13.4%YOY。 
成熟製程 NeoBit 佔 24.6%，+8.6%YOY。 
先進製程 NeoFuse 佔 70.7%，+12.3%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 1.5%，+927%YOY。 
MTP 佔 3.2%，+32.5%YOY。 
權利金成長預計將持續加速，主要受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產
品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組
合比重提高。

二、2026 年前三季授權金
+38.2% YOY，PUF - based 
+234% YOY 
 2026 前三季授權金營收 12.51 億元，+38.2%YOY。 
NeoBit 佔 11.9%，-39.3%YOY。 
NeoFuse 佔 36.5%，+35.1%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 31.7%，+234%YOY。 
MTP 佔 19.9%，+22.9%YOY。 
授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬體安
全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。

三、維持買進理由  1) 受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的
營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組合比重提高，權利金成長預計將
持續加速。 
2) 授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬
體安全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。 
3) PUF-based 已貢獻 2026 年前三季營收 12.6%，+252%YOY。應用面包括
CPU、Storage、光通、FPGA、高速傳輸晶片多面向。Arm Neoverse CSS V3
已將力旺的 PUF 列入核心元件。

자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

 외인지분율 45.16%  
 배당수익률 0.74%  
 주요주주 한화 외 6 인

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 (24.9) (6.2) (23.5)  
 상대 (13.1) (10.5) (47.4)  
 절대 (달러환산) (23.8) 1.5  (18.0)

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

## Document 263: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸HD현대중공업(329180) 3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸3Q26 Review_ 악재 소멸 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다. 
목표주가 (하향) 550,000원

## Document 268: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC 으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500 억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O 에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7 억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

Topics  Regulatory reform in India's insurance sector

Over the past quarter century, the Indian government has pursued the liberalization of the country's insurance market, 
with the aim of bringing stability to people's living standards and strengthening financial intermediation functions. 
Recently, however, it has begun to reverse course, tightening rules in order to better protect consumers.

■ India's insurance market has grown rapidly with deregulation 
The Indian government has long pursued 
the development of the country's insurance 
market, with the aim of bringing stability to 
people's living standards and strengthening 
financial intermediation functions. This 
stance can be attributed to two background 
factors:  
The first is that India's social security 
system remains underdeveloped. As a result, 
enrollment in health and life insurance can 
provide a vital safety net for mitigating the 
risk of sharp declines in household income 
due to unexpected illnesses or accidents.  
The second is that the sustainable 
development of the Indian economy requires 
the accumulation of long-term and stable 
domestic capital, separate from short-term 
bank deposits, in order to finance the 
development of infrastructure and the 
nurturing of industry. The hope has been that 
the enormous sums of money collected and 
managed by insurance companies can serve 
as a reliable source for this long-term 
financing.  
These factors have led the Indian 
government to walk a path of gradual 
deregulation over the past quarter century. 
The process began with the liberalization of 
market entry by private and foreign 
companies in 2000, which was followed by 
a lifting of the ban on "bancassurance" (the 
sale of insurance through bank branches) in 
2002, the raising of the ceiling on foreign 
ownership, and other reforms.  
This series of deregulatory measures 
spurred the entry of numerous private and foreign 
firms, causing market competition to intensify. Several 
Japanese insurers, including Nippon Life and the Tokio 
Marine Group, also entered the market, helping to drive 
investment in India.  
Besides deregulation related to market entry and 
sales, other factors also contributed to the rapid growth 
of India's insurance market, namely the expansion of 
the middle class, improvements in household financial 
literacy, and the digitalization of insurance services. 
Premium income grew at a pace exceeding economic 
growth, and in recent years insurance companies' assets 
under management have reached a level equivalent to 
Year Event
Establishment of the IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory
and Development Authority of India)
Acceptance of market entry applications from
domestic private firms and foreign entities
(foreign equity cap set at 26%)

3 
incentives to sell products and inappropriate sales practices. This should shore up the credibility of the 
insurance market and the foundation for its sustainable development. 
On the minus side, there are worries about side effects arising from the transition. As rules on selling 
become stricter, insurance companies and the financial institutions acting as insurance agents will inevitably 
see some temporary moderation in the pace of their earnings growth. Moreover, given that insurance 
companies and financial institutions are major investors in India's financial markets, tighter regulation is 
likely to exert downward pressure on equity and bond prices. 
These changes in the insurance market provide an important perspective for assessing the future 
trajectory of the Indian economy. They illustrate that rapid market expansion driven by liberalization can 
create a variety of problems. And this dynamic is not limited to the insurance sector, as it can also occur 
across many other industries. At the same time, the redesign and recalibration of regulations in response to 
such developments can provide a foundation for a new stage of growth. The new insurance regulations 
could constitute an early example of this phenomenon, so will be closely watched to see what changes they 
bring in the years ahead.  
  (Shotaro Kumagai)

Topics  Talent travel curbs may weigh on China’s growth

In September, the Chinese government introduced new exit-entry rules. Indicative of an effort to retain talent in order 
to prevent technology leakage, the rules could narrow opportunities for China to absorb overseas knowledge and weigh 
on its economic growth over the medium to long term.

■ Scope of overseas travel restrictions expanded 
On September 15, the Chinese 
government brought in new rules 
specifying conditions and screening 
procedures for entry and exit. The new 
rules explicitly state that the Ministry of 
Commerce or other departments may 
prohibit Chinese citizens from leaving the 
country if they violate export-control or 
technology import/export regulations and 
are deemed likely to threaten national 
industrial or technological security.  
Under China's Exit and Entry 
Administration Law, which came into 
effect in 2013, defendants and suspects in 
criminal cases, as well as persons involved 
in unsettled civil cases whom courts have 
prohibited from leaving the country, had 
already been subject to exit restrictions. In 
addition, Chinese citizens could be denied 
permission to leave the country if the 
competent departments determined that 
their departure might endanger national 
security or interests. Aside from formal exit 
bans, personnel who handle state secrets 
and officials in the public sector have long 
been subject to controls such as centralized 
passport management and the need to 
obtain approval in advance of overseas travel. What the new rules effectively do is make it clear that exit 
can also be restricted when there are issues from the standpoint of economic security. 
Against this backdrop, it was reported at the end of September that overseas travel restrictions already 
imposed on key personnel in the AI and semiconductor sectors had been expanded to include their spouses, 
children, and other family members. According to these reports, these key personnel include prominent AI 
startup founders, senior executives at major private enterprises, and core R&D talent. Although the measure 
does not constitute a blanket travel ban, affected individuals and their families are reportedly required to 
obtain government approval for exit even in the case of short private trips or business travel. 
This regulatory tightening is thought to be related to the technological rivalry between the U.S. and China. 
As competition surrounding critical technologies such as generative AI intensifies, the Chinese government 
appears to view not only technologies themselves, but also the researchers and engineers who create them, 
as strategic assets. By widening travel curbs to encompass family members, the authorities are likely 
seeking to prevent overseas emigration by individuals or the relocation of R&D operations abroad. A 
noteworthy aspect of the recent reports is that the controls apparently now extend beyond the public sector 
to key personnel at private enterprises, as well as their families. Restrictions like these on talent mobility 
could undermine the mechanisms of technology absorption and productivity improvement that have 
hitherto facilitated China's growth.

■ China's productivity gains have been supported by market opening and technology 
absorption 
Productivity exerts a substantial influence on 
economic growth. Total factor productivity (TFP), an 
indicator of an economy's overall production 
efficiency, provides an estimate of efficiency in 
production that cannot be explained solely in terms of 
inputs of capital and labor, and reflects factors such as 
levels of technology and efficiency of resource 
allocation. 
China's TFP has been rising for many years. In 
addition to the transition to a market economy and the 
growth of private enterprises since the country adopted 
the policy of reform and opening up, Chinese 
productivity has benefitted from the absorption of 
foreign technology and managerial know‑how through 
trade and direct investment. A labor shift from 
agriculture to manufacturing and other sectors, along 
with competition among firms and the weeding out of 
weaker players, has also helped deliver productivity 
gains across the economy.  
Nevertheless, China's TFP remains low relative to 
other major economies. According to estimates from 
Penn World Table, China's TFP in 2023 was less than 
half that of the U.S., and it was also below the levels not 
only of Germany and other major European economies 
and of South Korea and Japan, but of Thailand and 
Malaysia, too. Factors behind this include inefficient 
resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced 
technologies and management practices. Preferential 
financial and institutional treatment for state‑owned 
enterprises, regional protectionism, and mechanisms 
that impede market entry and exit have distorted the 
allocation of resources, preventing capital and labor 
from being fully utilized by more productive firms and sectors. Moreover, although China has achieved 
strong competitiveness in certain advanced industries, such as electric vehicles and semiconductors, the 
leading-edge technologies and management practices developed in these sectors have not sufficiently 
diffused to the service sector and small and medium‑sized enterprises, which form the foundation of the 
economy, and this has constrained overall productivity growth.  
■ Moves to contain technology could hinder growth over the medium to long term 
Efforts to retain talent in order to prevent technology leakage could further delay China's productivity 
catch‑up with advanced economies. While restrictions on overseas travel by elite professionals may help 
with the retention of domestic R&D talent and protection of current technological advantages, they could 
also reduce opportunities to absorb overseas knowledge and to jointly develop new technologies with 
foreign partners. The transfer of technology and know‑how does not occur solely through the sharing of 
"explicit knowledge," in the form of academic papers, technical blueprints, and so on. "Tacit knowledge," 
which is transmitted through face‑to‑face discussion, collaborative work, and the acquisition of experience 
in production settings, is also crucial. If travel controls delay or prevent participation in international 
conferences, joint research projects, and technology exchanges with overseas companies, opportunities to 
absorb such knowledge may be lost, slowing technological innovation and improvements in production 
processes. Furthermore, if obtaining travel approval becomes more time‑consuming or less predictable, the 
overseas assignment or international deployment of technical personnel could also be obstructed. 
The new rules also cover the entry of foreign nationals. If uncertainty surrounding the administration of 
entry and exit controls increases, it could create obstacles to attracting and retaining highly skilled talent 
from abroad. At a time when inefficient resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced knowledge 
are already weighing on productivity growth in China, curbs on people-to- people exchange could further

impede both the absorption and creation of knowledge, as well as its practical application. Although the 
magnitude of the impact will depend on the scope of application and actual implementation on the ground, 
restrictions on talent exchanges and overseas travel that reflect excessive emphasis on technology 
protection could slow China's TFP growth over the medium to long term and act as a headwind to the 
Chinese economy by lowering its potential growth rate. 
(Hiromu Komiya)

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -16.26 điểm tương ứng -0.93%, với thanh khoản mở 
rộng. Thiết bị dụng cụ Y tế, Tiện ích khí và Bảo hiểm là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 
-870 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã MBB, VIC và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
ở mức 504 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -2,010 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -10,888 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 1,208 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -4,070 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần giảm điểm thứ 2 liên tiếp khi VN-Index lùi về kiểm định vùng hỗ trợ 
quanh 1.725 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm giảm hơn 30 điểm trước khi thu hẹp 
đà giảm nhờ lực cầu ở nhóm chứng khoán. Áp lực bán tập trung ở nhóm vốn hóa lớn, đặc 
biệt là bộ đôi Vingroup (VIC, VHM) cùng một số cổ phiếu ngân hàng và công nghệ, khiến 
chỉ số thiếu lực đỡ. Giao dịch của khối ngoại diễn biến trái chiều và giằng co — có phiên 
mua ròng trở lại ở một số mã, nhưng vẫn xen kẽ bán ròng ở nhóm trụ như VHM, VCB, cho 
thấy dòng vốn ngoại chưa xác lập xu hướng rõ ràng. Bối cảnh bên ngoài kém thuận lợi khi 
nhóm cổ phiếu công nghệ tại Mỹ bị bán tháo, kéo theo các chỉ số chính toàn cầu đồng 
loạt lao dốc. Mặc dù vậy, kỳ vọng vào động thái mở kênh hoán đổi ngoại tệ của NHNN để 
hỗ trợ thanh khoản, hạ lãi suất đã giúp tâm lý nhà đầu tư trong nước ổn định và không 
xuất hiện áp lực bán tháo.

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến spinning thân nhỏ với bóng trên/dưới khá dài, 
cùng thanh khoản gia tăng, thể hiện sự giằng co mạnh nhưng về tổng thể lại cho thấy diễn 
biến đã cân bằng trở lại sau tuần giảm điểm trước đó. Trên khung ngày, mặc dù trải qua 
các phiên biến động tăng/giảm mạnh đan xen nhưng việc cho phản ứng bật tăng tích cực 
tại quanh đường biên dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn cho thấy tương quan 
vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều hướng tăng. Diễn biến phân hóa và biến động mạnh có thể còn 
tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ tiếp tục hướng lên thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
It has been an action-packed few weeks for markets. The US
2026 mid-term elections saw a repudiation of President Trump
with the Democrats now set to take control over both houses
of Congress. The outcome and easing Middle East tensions

saw Brent crude oil fall from just under US$120/bbl to roughly
US$95/bbl, back around the post-Iran-conflict average. The
associated easing in inflation fears has supported a continued
descent in bond yields and rally in risk sentiment – albeit with
significant ‘wrinkles’ around a high US inflation read and an
AI-led pull-back in equity markets that has been compounded
by the sudden resignation of NVIDIA’s CEO.
Many central banks have already begun re-tightening policy

– with the Bank of Canada and Bank of England the latest
additions to the pack – but what comes next will differ widely
across jurisdictions. In the US, the combination of strong
activity and sticky inflation have led FOMC members to more
strongly advocate for further tightening. We have added a
January hike to the December move already in our forecasts,
before a long pause and eventual cuts in 2028.
Europe shares has similar inflation concerns but not so much

strength on the growth front. The ECB sees activity near
trend and is uncomfortable with inflation at 3.8%yr, so we
expect a December hike as insurance. Unlike market pricing
though, we think this will be the last, with energy and
investment-related price pressures easing through 2027 and
scope to ease back towards neutral in 2028. China faces a
very different challenge – not too much demand but too little.
Industrial strength and technology investment are providing

support but we expect policy direction and stimulus will be
needed to turn household demand, with this expected to
come in 2027 and property the most likely catalyst.
The RBA meanwhile followed its September hike with another
in November, but the Governor’s call to ‘take stock’ signals a
December pause and we think the hiking phase is now more
clearly over. We continue to pencil in a move to cuts in the
second half of 2027. While a February hike is not completely

out of the question, policy is clearly restrictive and downside
risks are crystallising. Reflecting this, we have downgraded
our GDP, labour market and housing market forecasts for 2027.
Australia: The housing downturn has been sharper than
expected and is set to spill over more materially to the broader
economy. The scale of these spillovers should become clearer
over coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside
risks to growth build. Business investment remains a source

of resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but rising
costs and growing community opposition have seen some
projects shelved. Still, the supply-side picture is constructive,
with strong investment in the market sector driving capital
deepening and productivity gains, albeit uneven ones.
Commodities: The commodities complex was a story of two
halves in October, with sharp gains across energy offsetting
weakness elsewhere and lifting our broadest commodities

index 0.6%mth. Reflecting continued energy-market tightness,
we expect Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 and have raised
our Q4 Japanese LNG forecast to US$27.
Global FX Markets: Of late, risks have favoured the US
dollar and FOMC member concerns have been decidedly one-
sided towards higher inflation. The DXY has consequently
continued to test the upper limit of its 18-month range, but
remains inside it.
New Zealand: A busy month has included a tight – and

currently inconclusive – general election, a surprise hold by the
RBNZ in October and a somewhat disappointing Q3 labour
market report. However, the medium-term outlook remains little
changed, with a recovering economy and above-target inflation
likely to lead to further monetary tightening in coming months.
Pacific: Distance from major markets, small domestic
economies and a heavy reliance on imported fuel and food
mean global disruptions tend to reach the Pacific at a higher

cost. However, most Pacific countries face the latest shock
with somewhat better buffers.
United States: Data suggests the US carried strong
momentum in to H2 2026. But questions are being asked over
the profitability of AI deployment, household real incomes are
trending flat to down, and term interest rates continue to weigh.
China: China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3, keeping
authorities on track for 2026, but more importantly offering a
little more time to accelerate domestic demand in to 2027.

Asia: We expect the BoJ to raise its policy rate by 25bps to
1.50% in December, before gradually taking it to 2.0% through
2027. The case for further tightening rests on the evidence that
firms are more willing to raise prices, labour scarcity supports
persistent wage growth, which should in turn support spending.
Europe & UK: The ECB left its key policy rates unchanged in
October. Another hike is forecast in December. Against market
pricing, we expect this will prove the last for the current cycle.

Taking stock

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
All major central banks are now in a rate-hiking phase. The
stance of policy nonetheless varies considerably across
economies, as does the policy outlook.
The Bank of England and Bank of Canada increased their
policy rates for the first time this cycle and the RBA a fifth time

since the last Market Outlook. The pace of increase is generally
moderate: the FOMC and RBNZ paused at their latest
meetings – the RBNZ unexpectedly so – but both are expected
to lift policy rates further. Policy is likely still accommodative
in New Zealand and Canada, consistent with the economic and
labour market slack evident in these economies. In Australia it
is clearly restrictive, as discussed below.
The US is contending with sticky inflation, as evidenced by

recent CPI and labour market data. We have long been of the
view that the US is likely to be structurally more inflationary
than many peers, and concerns about inflation are increasingly
coming to the fore in FOMC members’ comments. Accordingly
we have added another hike in January to our US rates view, on
top of the one in December already forecast (see page 20).
The main, but not sole, driver of the pivot to tightening has been
the energy price shock. That said, exclusion-based core inflation

measures (excluding food and energy) are also above central
bank targets. Market pricing of future central bank moves had
been sensitive to oil prices and the outlook in the Middle East
earlier in the year. That correlation broke down during the bond
sell-off, which was mostly a phenomenon of real yields, but
seems to have  re-emerged in the pricing for policy in 2027.
Spot and daated Brent oil prices have fallen markedly in recent
days as tensions have eased and negotiations appear to have

restarted. This has not affected very near-term pricing of central
bank decisions, and an initial moderation of mid-2027 pricing
retraced after the strong US CPI print and some Fed speeches.
Further along the yield curve, last month’s sell-off in bond
markets has largely reversed. Unlike the increase in yields,
when longer-run market-based measures of inflation
expectations relatively stable, the more recent rally has
involved some decline in these expectations measures. Most of

the adjustment has been front-loaded: the 1-year expectations
derived from swaps have declined 45bps for Australia and
nearly 60bps for the US since the beginning of November.
However, inflation expectations derived for future years have
also moderated. The 5-year-5-year forward measures derived
from swaps and breakevens have both declined, with the latter
noticeably below the 2.5% RBA target midpoint. The same
measures for the US have also declined over this period, but by

less, and remain further above the Fed’s 2% target, consistent
with our view about inflation pressures there.
Global asset market rotation ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... while the RBA nears a pause
The rally in long bond markets has occurred at the same time as
equity markets have tended to sell off, particularly in the tech
sector. Another wave of scepticism around the sustainability
of the data centre build-out has formed and found expression

in the equity prices of many semiconductor manufacturers and
other related firms. One possible trigger for the latest sell-off
may have been the release of Anthropic’s prospectus ahead
of its IPO. As well as the more existential risks flagged in the
documents, its stated plans for further data centre investment
have concentrated focus on the scale of the data centre and AI
boom more broadly, and the enormous funding task involved.
The RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) hiked at its November

meeting following up the late September hike as expected. As
we noted at the time, the MPB was responding to the apparent
crystallisation of some of the risks that it had flagged back in
August. In particular, the re-emergence of pressures on fuel
prices led to fears of a further wave of pass-through of energy
costs into the prices of other goods and services. The Q3
outcome for underlying inflation outstripped expectations and
Q4 is also likely to be uncomfortably high. With inflation already

materially above target, the RBA is worried that inflation
expectations could be rising, pointing to liaison and business
survey evidence. So far, though, other business surveys suggest
that pass-through has been less severe than the original wave
of price increases at the outbreak of the Middle East conflict.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor flagged
that it was a good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening
to date. Both we and the market read this as a signal that the

MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote
at the meeting also suggests that some external members are
more attentive to downside risks than the internal members.
We think the most likely outcome from here is that the RBA
has ended its rate-hiking phase, and that a cutting phase will
begin in the second half of 2027. There is still a risk of a hike
in February, and we believe the MPB will at least discuss the
option at that meeting. The staff and senior officials remain

sensitive to inflation risks, and have a downbeat view of
supply capacity growth. The latter meant that the RBA had to
upgrade its forecasts for the labour force participation rate in
its However, policy is clearly restrictive, and while there are
upside risks to inflation, there are clearly downside risks, too.
With markets now expecting that the RBA is likely at the end
of its hiking phase, while most peers are expected to have 2–3
hikes to go, short-end yield differentials have narrowed. This

has been reflected in the AUD exchange rate, which has tended
to underperform in recent days. We have tempered our near-
term outlook for the AUD, but note that the TWI remains abourt
4% above the 2022–2024 average, and that this appreciation
will be contributing to the disinflationary impact of tight policy.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
The housing downturn has been sharper than expected and
is set to spill over more materially to the broader economy.
The scale of these spillovers should become clearer over
coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside risks

to growth build. Business investment remains a source of
resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but signs
of strain are emerging, with rising costs and growing
community opposition seeing some projects shelved. The
2026 Annual National Accounts offered a more constructive
supply-side picture, with strong investment across parts of
the market sector driving capital deepening and supporting
productivity, but this has been uneven.
As set out on page 26, we expect the lift in inflation this year

to subside through 2027, with underlying measures easing
back from 3.7%yr currently to 2.7%yr by end 2027 and 2.3%yr
in 2028. Indeed, the deeper housing downturn and associated
spillovers give us greater conviction in this view. The
correction is now the largest nominal price decline on records
going back to 1980 and appears to have accelerated following
the November 25bp RBA rate hike. This is on the back of
dismal dwelling finance approvals outcome which showed

the value of new loans plunged 15.5%qtr in Q3, the biggest
quarterly decline on records going back to 1991.
This downturn has some clear direct effects. The housing
sector, covering both real estate transactions and residential
construction, accounts for around 5–6% of GDP. The drop
in turnover will take about 0.4ppts off GDP growth over
calendar 2026 (captured through the “ownership transfer
costs” component of expenditure GDP) with the downturn also

expected to have a significant drag on dwelling construction
heading into 2027.
Steepest housing downturn in almost 50 years ...
Pat Bustamante, Senior Economist
However, the impact goes well beyond these direct effects.
Falling housing wealth will weigh on household spending,
while the residential building has strong linkages to the rest of
the economy. Residential investment supports activity across
manufacturing, transport and professional services; dollar

for dollar, we estimate its broader economic impact is around
1.5 times that of consumer spending. The flow on impacts via
employment and broader confidence are also substantive.
We have lowered our forecasts for household consumption,
ownership transfer costs and dwelling investment.
Consumption growth is now expected to trough at 1.1%yr
during 2027, from 1.8%yr forecast in our October Market
Outlook, before recovering to 2.7%yr in 2028. Ownership

transfer costs are expected to see a 25%yr decline over 2026,
the sharpest decline since the March quarter 1990, even
larger than what we saw during the pandemic. New dwelling
investment is expected to contract by 1.0%yr in 2027, rather
than grow by 2.1%yr as we expected back in October, before
recovering by 3.2%yr in 2028, down from 4.9%yr. Together
with some flow-on dampening effect on employment, these
revisions lower our GDP growth forecast to just 1.0%yr in

2027, from 1.6%yr, and 2.1%yr in 2028, from 2.5%yr.
We do not expect the slowdown to become immediately
apparent in the data. Consumer spending looks to have been
relatively resilient through the September quarter. Discretionary
services spend has been notably firm in particular, with only
some of this due to a catch-up on travel and tourism spend
disrupted by the Middle East conflict earlier in the year.
Household disposable incomes were still relatively well

supported through the quarter with some lift coming from tax
cuts and the FWC award wage increase. However, our card
tracker data suggests discretionary spending momentum has
faded noticeable over the first half of the December quarter.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
... to drive a sharp economic slowdown ...
The confidence impacts from housing downturn may also be
starting to show. While the drop in consumer sentiment back
to extreme lows has more to do with rate rises and fuel prices,
the weakening in business confidence became more prominent

in October with a notably sharp fall in forward orders. While
this has not had a flow-on impact on employment and capex
expectations to date, these will likely soften if confidence
remains negative and the signal from forward order starts to
be reflected in wider business conditions.
Business investment is still likely to be an important offset, with
data centres and renewables supporting activity. But even here,
some cracks are starting to emerge. Reports of project delays

in NSW and South Australia amid community opposition pose
limited risk to projects already underway but could increasingly
affect the pipeline of proposed data-centre investment (with
the notably cancellation of an IPO for a local operator as well).
Sentiment across the AI sector more broadly has also weakened
with equity valuations dropping, leadership instability at NVIDIA.
Anthropic’s ‘mega IPO’ due November 18 and expected to land
at market valuation of ~US$1trn will be an important test of how

fundamental the shift in sentiment may have been.
Annual National Accounts
The Annual National Accounts (the ‘Annuals’) provide updated
benchmarks and some useful detail including on investment
trends across industries, the capital stock and productivity.
The 2025–26 figures show somewhat firmer economic
activity and business investment than previously estimated,
particularly in 2024–25. The economy grew by 2.3% in
year-average terms, broadly in line with the initial quarterly

National Accounts estimate of 2.4%. However, growth in
2024–25 was revised up by 0.3ppts to 1.6%, largely reflecting
stronger private business investment. Business investment
is now estimated to have increased by 1.1% in 2024–25,
compared with the initial estimate of a 0.2% decline. The
revisions do not materially alter the broader productivity story,
but they do suggest that the economy was growing a little
more strongly but with the same degree of inflation in 2025.

The Annuals also provide a richer picture of the supply side,
including investment by industry and the evolution of the capital
stock, details that are not available in the quarterly accounts.
These data point to a notable lift in investment in the market (ex-
mining) part of the economy, where investment has gone from
around 10% of nominal GDP in to just over 11.5%. Outside of
this, investment has been softer, particularly in the mining sector.
Within the market sector, investment by the IT industry,

increasingly focused on data centres, has risen to its highest
share of nominal GDP since the technology boom of the
1990s. Utilities investment, including renewables and
associated infrastructure, is stronger still, at its highest share
since 1983, while investment by the professional services
industry has reached a record high. Across the non-market
sector (including health, education and public administration),
investment has also held at a slightly higher share of the

economy, driven by the health and public administration
(including defence) industries.
The investment upswing is also beginning to translate into
capital deepening as industries look to rebuild capital stocks
after a period of soft growth. Outside mining, market-sector
capital per hour worked is at a record high and well above its pre-
pandemic level. That is an encouraging development, particularly
given that the sector accounts for 70% of total employment.

There are important divergences beneath the aggregate. Mining
continues to experience capital shallowing, as investment has
been insufficient to cover wear and tear and capital requirements
associated with additional workers. By contrast, the capital stock
in the non-market sector appears to have caught up with the
surge in employment we have seen since the pandemic, with
capital per worker increasing for the first time since the pandemic.

### Page 8

8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
Capital deepening will help support productivity growth
across the economy. The Annuals show that productivity in the
market sector (excluding mining) continues to grow by around
1% in year-average terms, while mining remains a drag on

aggregate productivity (down 0.8% in 2025–26 and almost
30% since 2017).
At the same time, a major recent headwind is easing as the rapid
expansion of the non-market sector slows. This matters for
aggregate productivity. Productivity growth reflects both gains
within industries, through capital deepening, technology and
better processes, and the ‘reallocation’ of resources between
industries with different productivity levels.
Measured productivity in the non-market sector is considerably

lower than across much of the market sector, partly reflecting
measurement issues (output is generally not well measured,
particularly when there are quality changes). As employment
shifted towards these industries, this compositional effect
weighed on aggregate productivity. Our estimates suggest it
has reduced the level of aggregate productivity by a significant
1ppt over the past 15 years and almost 0.3ppts over the three
years to 2025–26.
What drove growth in market-sector productivity?

It wasn’t only capital deepening that drove productivity growth.
Multifactor productivity (MFP), which captures how efficiently
businesses combine labour and capital through changes in
processes, technology, know-how, was the dominant driver of
market-sector productivity growth this year. This gives us more
conviction that the supply side is normalising, and productivity
growth will improve from its recent lows.
At the industry level, some of those recording stronger

investment are also beginning to deliver stronger productivity
outcomes, although the picture remains uneven.
... as non-mining sector rebuilds capital, productivity
IT stands out at the positive end of the spectrum, alongside
improving outcomes in manufacturing, administrative and
support services, and transport. These are also industries
where the diffusion of AI and other digital technologies could
deliver further productivity gains. Utilities and professional

services should also benefit as stronger investment translates
into greater capital intensity and efficiency.
At the other end, mining remains a major drag, consistent
with the capital shallowing evident in the investment and
capital-stock data. Construction is another notable laggard,
with productivity continuing to deteriorate despite strong
demand from housing, population growth, renewable-
energy infrastructure and data-centre projects. Construction

productivity fell around 2% in 2025–26 and almost 21% over
the 13 years from 2012–13 to 2025–26.
Bottom line
The Annuals provide a more constructive supply-side picture
than the aggregate productivity figures alone suggest. Market-
sector productivity outside mining continues to advance, the
reallocation drag from the expanding non-market sector looks
likely to fade, and capital deepening is strengthening across
important parts of the economy. The strong MFP outcome in

the market sector gives us more conviction that conviction that
the supply side is normalising, and productivity growth will
improve going ahead.
The critical question is whether the strong investment
underway in technology, professional services, electricity and
related infrastructure translates into stronger productivity
growth as that new capital is put to work. If it does, the
improvement in capital intensity evident in the Annuals
could mark the beginning of a more durable lift in Australia’s

productivity performance.
